ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u6	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to present a report to the Committee on Public Finance on the draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws together with draft implementing acts, print Nos 991 and 1192. This work took place both in the subcommittee on financial institutions and in the committee itself. They were long hours. The subject matter of this bill is extremely complicated, especially since it still involves, please, in addition to the amendments, the implementation of EU legislation, including financial instruments. For an average person, these things are rather incomprehensible, although I honestly admit that even for financial law professionals many of these records are so complicated that you have to read them several times to figure out what the author meant. Because as you know, it's not about who's making the law, it's about who's preparing the law. Most often here, I would say, we can find the cause of such excessive legislation that we are observing, especially as regards EU legislation. This is, in our view, one of the main problems of the Union - the phenomenon of some hyperinflation of law, i.e. alleged legal order, which actually complicates the legal system. In addition, we have practically every day new regulations, directives, opinions, recommendations, and all this must be included in national legislation. Often, ladies and gentlemen, there is very serious money and great financial interests of all kinds of lobby behind such EU directives. I ask you, when going to prints no. 991 and 1192, it must be said that the meaning of all these actions were changes in Polish legislation on the 2014 Act on the amendment of the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. They abolished the requirement to register derivatives in a CSD system. These derivatives are extremely complex, you can say, financial-bank products, today also called weapons of mass destruction, financial weapons of mass destruction, or famous derivatives. Such a form of a kind of casino, betting - practically everything. As Paul Volcker used to say, former head of the Fed, derivatives brought the world closer to disaster. They were one of the causes of the big financial crisis in 2008. I ask you, the need, the purpose of the proposed law was to abolish the division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market, because as a result of subsequent changes in the rules on the functioning of the domestic capital market, in particular the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, there has been practically a reduction in differences in the functioning of the stock market and the over-the-counter market. The purpose of the draft law is therefore to abolish the unjustified division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market at the moment, and this regulation will result in the inclusion of all the listed types of that market in the same draft regulated market. The aim is also to regulate the institution's derivatives account. It is also a matter of ensuring that the Financial Supervision Authority has adequate access to information about all organised derivatives and derivatives admitted to trading. It will therefore provide the supervisory authority with effective supervisory tools to enable it to properly assess its trading in financial instruments. This also implies a change in the authority issuing the permit to hold the stock exchange. The consequences of the proposed changes will also be important changes in the provisions of other laws, starting with the Commercial Companies Code, through the Trade Exchanges Act, bankruptcy law, investment fund law, and the Public Offering Act. We can say that we will practically have to notice major changes in a significant number of provisions. The work was exhausting, actually it could be said that it was going without more confrontation. And that's where I'd like to end it. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. Abolition of division... As a result of subsequent changes in the rules on the functioning of the national capital market over the last few years, the differences in the functioning of the stock market and the OTC market have been actually overcome. Similarly, Union law does not provide for such a distinction between regulated market operators. At the same time, the definition of a regulated market as an OTC market may mislead about his character. This is due to the fact that the term "over-the-counter market' is not reflected in the commonly functioning nomenclature resulting from European rules transposed by Member States into national legal systems. The purpose of the bill is therefore to abolish the unjustified division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market at present. Regulation will result in the inclusion of these types of markets in the same regulatory market concept. Ladies and gentlemen! The consequences of the proposed changes are also the need to equip the supervisory authority with effective supervisory tools to ensure effective supervision of the safety and regularity of secondary trading of financial instruments, in particular to determine the scope of investments carried out by entities not operating in the territory of the Republic of Poland, which are parties to stock exchange transactions and which are not obliged under the regulations in force in Poland to record transactions concluded on derivatives on any accounts. To this end, the draft law provides for the power of the Financial Supervision Commission to apply directly to these entities for relevant information. At the same time, it is necessary to stress that the changes envisaged in this respect do not affect the scope of supervision of investment firms at European level in the field of capital market law. Ladies and gentlemen! In addition, the proposed solution will eliminate the significant interpretation doubts identified in the administrative procedures to date for the authorisation of stock exchanges. Practical application of the provisions of Article. 25 of the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments leads to the conclusion that, after the Commission has forwarded a request to the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions for the authorisation of holding a stock exchange, in the event of a change of this proposal during the administrative procedure conducted by the Minister, there is no legal basis for asking the committee to give an opinion on the changes made to it. It may therefore appear that the opinion of the committee will be unrelated to the facts in the event that the party makes substantial changes to the proposal after it has been forwarded by the committee to the Minister responsible for financial institutions. Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, please accept the proposed amendments and I would like to make amendments on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u10	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and certain other laws. Over the past few years, further changes have been made to the rules that have concerned the functioning of the domestic capital market, which has consequently led to the elimination of differences in the functioning of the stock market and the over-the-counter market. It is therefore appropriate to abolish the division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market. The actual differences between markets are not relevant enough to consider it justified to further distinguish between two types of regulated market. Distinctions to the stock market and over-the-counter market are also not provided for by EU law. In addition, the definition of a regulated market as an OTC market may be misleading as to its nature. The concept of over-the-counter market is not reflected in the functioning terminology that results from European legislation. The introduction of the proposed amendments will require the adaptation of the provisions of other laws: the Act on Accounting, the Act on Paid Motorways and the National Road Fund, but also the Act on Energy Law, the Act on Commercial Companies Code, the Act on Trade Exchanges, the Act on Insolvency Law, the Act on Investment Funds and the Management of Alternative Investment Funds, the Act on Public Offering and Conditions of Introduction of Financial Instruments to Organized Trading, and on Public Companies, as well as the Act on Supervision of the Financial Market. High Izbo! The aim of the project is to abolish the division of the regulated market into the stock market and over-the-counter market, and as a result of their inclusion in the general concept of the regulated market. According to this project, the company that currently operates the stock exchange market, the Warsaw Stock Exchange SA, and the company that operates the OTC market, BondSpot SA, will become companies operating two separate regulated markets. The draft provides for appropriate transitional periods for the adjustment of both companies' activities. In addition, the project envisages the introduction of rules on the institution's derivatives account. The new regulations will define the entities authorised to keep records of derivatives, clarify the scope of the authorisations to keep such records, while identifying the records on which derivatives are to be recorded, and specify the rules for determining the holders of the derivative, the rules for transferring rights and obligations from certain derivatives. The new regulations will also facilitate the Financial Supervision Commission's access to information on derivatives and the activities of individual investors in the trading of these financial instruments. The project also envisages a change in the authority which issues permits to conduct the stock exchange market, from the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions to the Financial Supervision Commission. With this change it will shorten, we hope, the process of licensing stock exchanges. The Financial Supervision Commission will therefore be the competent authority to withdraw the authorisation to hold the stock exchange and to prohibit the conduct of the regulated market. High Izbo, the Civic Platform Club is in favour of this report. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u12	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a position regarding the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws, prints No. 991 and 1192. The amendment of the law provides for three fundamental amendments. The first concerns the abolition of the division of the regulated market into the stock market and over-the-counter market. The current distribution of the regulated market was based on the assumption that the OTC market, due to greater formal freedom and lower requirements for companies wishing to issue their securities, would be intended for small and medium-sized enterprises. However, the unification of the rules in subsequent years has caused clear differences between the two types of regulated market to be blurred. So closing the time limits on the stock market and over-the-counter market is an idea that we can support. The proposed changes are also a step towards adapting the Polish law to EU rules, which do not provide in the commonly functioning nomenclature for the concept of the OTC market. Another proposed amendment is the introduction of rules on the institution's derivatives account. The Financial Supervision Commission will be granted new powers and will be able to request information on investments in Poland by non-business entities in Poland which are party to stock exchange transactions and are not required to record transactions on derivatives on any accounts. The third change envisaged by the project's authors is the change of the authority issuing the authorisation to operate the regulated market. So far, he has been Minister of Finance. The novel deprives him of this right, while imposing an obligation related to it on the Commission for Financial Supervision. Ladies and gentlemen! Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Modern Club hopes that the announced changes and strengthening of the role of the Financial Supervision Commission and the harmonisation of the market will not adversely affect the functioning of the stock exchange sector of financial instruments and all those present. All our doubts about this matter were not resolved during the committee work. We recommend this draft report and support it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u14	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. These are 991 and 1192 prints. The draft law abolishes the division of the regulated market in financial instruments into the stock market and over-the-counter market and provides for both types of market to be covered by the same concept of regulated market. Years ago, the OTC market was a market, it seems, less formalized and with lower requirements for issuers. The differences involved another entity issuing the market authorisation, namely the stock market was the responsibility of the Minister of Finance and the OTC market was the responsibility of the Financial Supervision Commission. The next difference is the value of the existing equity capital - in listed companies it is the amount of 10 million, in the OTC market it is the amount of 5 million. And the third difference is another legal basis for the provision of current and periodic information by issuers of securities. The differences I mention, in fact, as a result of the changes to the rules introduced so far have been blurred, and hence the harmonisation of the rules on the functioning of the regulated market is an appropriate process, fully justified. The work on the project continued both in the subcommittee for financial institutions and in the Committee on Public Finances, and before that it was also consulted. The project, although quite extensive in its content, is actually a technical project with proposals for uniform solutions for the entire regulated market. According to the draft, the authority empowered to issue the authorisation and to prohibit the conduct of the regulated market is the Financial Supervision Commission. It is the KNF that is also equipped with numerous supervisory tools to ensure the supervision of the safety and regularity of trading of financial instruments. In addition, in order to ensure the safety of trading and the supervision of the forward market, the proposed law provides for the introduction of a derivatives account in which derivatives admitted to trading will be recorded. It should also be noted that the project contains solutions that already exist in the Member States of the European Union. Thus, in summary, the Act should contribute to reducing the number of procedures, reducing the number of documents relating to the conduct of a regulated market by one common procedure, and to shortening the authorisation process and the use of transparent supervisory tools. In general, the project is well founded, it also includes numerous laws affected by this subject, and my club, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, supports the project discussed today. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u23	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u24	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The primary objective of the draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and certain other laws is to implement into the Polish legal order Directive 2014/57/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on criminal sanctions for market abuse. In addition, the aim of the project is to enable the application of Regulation (EU) No 909/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 July 2014 on improving securities settlement in the European Union and on central securities depositories. The MAD Directive and the MAR Regulation replace Directive 2003/6/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 28 January 2003 on insider dealing and market manipulation. The general objective of the new regulation is to enhance investor protection by ensuring market integrity, a coherent approach to abuse and a level playing field, which is to be achieved by updating and harmonising the catalogues for regulation of financial instruments and markets, strengthening the powers of supervisory authorities to conduct market abuse proceedings, and limiting the option and freedom of Member States to formulate appropriate rules, thereby strengthening the dissuasive nature of market abuse penalties, as the rapporteur said. According to the MAD, criminal sanctions for the most serious abuses are to be imposed on both natural persons and legal persons, with legal persons subject to sanctions covering both fines and non-criminal fines. The MAR Regulation introduces and clarifies the definition of market manipulation and confidential information, while regulating what is the use of confidential information and in which cases the use of information is authorised. In connection with the implementation of the MAD Directive and the possibility of the application of the MAR Regulation, the draft law also provides for amendments to certain other legal acts, such as the Banking Law, the Trade Exchange Act, the Corporate Liability Act for acts prohibited under penalty, the Investment Funds Act and the Alternative Investment Fund Management Act, and the Capital Market Surveillance Act and the Financial Market Surveillance Act. The proposal does not require it to be submitted to the bodies and institutions of the European Union in order to obtain an opinion, notification, consultation or agreement. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. The Law and Justice Club will therefore support this bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u25	Thank you very much. I invite Mrs Monica Wielichowska, the Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u26	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I will present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws, this is print No. 1097. High Izbo! The purpose of the draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws is to implement into the Polish legal order the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on Criminal Sanctions for Market Abuse - it is the MAD Directive, the Market Abuse Directive - and thus to implement within the framework of national law the objectives set by the EU legislature. The regulations contained in the proposal will also serve to apply the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on market abuse, which is the MAR Regulation. Furthermore, the aim of the project is to enable the application of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on improving securities settlement in the European Union and on central securities depositories. The project will enhance the security of securities settlement systems that operate in the Union and improve their efficiency. The MAD Directive and the MAR Regulation replace the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 28 January 2003 on insider dealing and market manipulation. The aim of the new regulation is primarily to enhance investor protection while ensuring market integrity, a coherent approach to abuse and a level playing field, which is to be achieved by updating and clarifying the catalogue of financial instruments and markets; strengthening the powers of supervisors to conduct market abuse proceedings; limiting the option and freedom of Member States to formulate appropriate rules and thereby strengthening the dissuasive nature of penalties for market abuse. As a result of the revision of Directive 2003/6/EC, it has been noted that not all competent national authorities had at their disposal a full range of powers that would enable them to respond to market abuse through appropriate sanctions. In particular, not all Member States have provided for administrative fines for insider dealing and market manipulation. In order to eliminate fraud that undermines the integrity of financial markets and investor confidence in financial instruments as well as in the market itself, the European legislator has decided that criminal sanctions for fraud in the financial market will be regulated at the level of the MAD. In particular, that Directive obliges Member States to impose criminal sanctions to a maximum extent of at least 4 years of imprisonment for the use of confidential information, to recommend or to encourage the use of confidential information and to manipulate the financial market and to impose criminal sanctions to a maximum extent of at least 2 years of imprisonment for unlawful disclosure of confidential information. The MAR Regulation introduces and clarifies the definitions of market manipulation and confidential information, while regulating what is the use of confidential information and in which cases the use of information is authorised. High Izbo! The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u32	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government bill amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws, prints no. 1097 and 1266. The project under investigation is the implementation of EU law, it is the implementation of the so-called. the MAD Directive on criminal sanctions for market abuse and the European regulation called the MAR Regulation, which serves on capital market abuse. The draft law also includes new provisions on the application of the Regulation on improving securities settlement in the European Union and on central securities depositories. It is called the CSDR regulation in short. The fundamental objectives of the amended law are, of course, to strengthen the protection of participants in the Polish capital market, including individual investors in public companies, and to increase the safety of trading on the capital market. In order to eliminate fraud that undermines the integrity of financial markets and investor confidence in financial instruments as well as in the market itself, it was decided that criminal sanctions for fraud would be regulated at the level of the MAD. In connection with the implementation of the MAD, the Polish legislation increased the penalty from 3 to 4 years of imprisonment for disclosure of confidential information, the provision of recommendations or the encouragement to acquire or dispose of the financial instruments concerned. On the other hand, the direct application of the MAR Regulation has resulted in maximum administrative fines for non-compliance with that Regulation. They are very, drastically high against the Polish law so far. The draft of this Act, the first reading of which took place on 13 December at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finances, was analysed in detail by the subcommittee on financial institutions. It was then considered that the financial penalties applied initially are very high and do not conform to our Polish conditions. The subcommittee therefore proposed a reduction in the level of penalties, which was logical and appropriate. To be honest, yesterday before the committee meeting, the project wasn't exactly shocking. Unfortunately, during the work of the Committee on Public Finance yesterday, the reduced penalty rates proposed by the subcommittee were changed to the amount originally proposed. The CSDR requires Member States to have supervisory powers under national law to impose these high penalties. In the case of a natural person, it may be up to EUR 5 million, i.e. 20 670 thousand, in the case of a legal person - up to a maximum of EUR 20 million, or 82 680 thousand. zł. Similarly, the Public Finance Commission has also restored sanctions resulting from the MAR Regulation. Here it is slightly lower, because these sanctions for a natural person are at least EUR 1 million - of course the maximum ones - i.e. more than PLN 4 million, in the case of a legal person - 2.5 EUR million, or more than PLN 10 million. These rates are valid in all Member States of the European Union. These are the maximum rates, as I stressed, which may be imposed by the Financial Supervision Authority for fraud committed, but the committee is obliged to take account of, inter alia, the sanctions. the seriousness of the infringement and the financial situation of the entity to which the penalty is imposed. However, I still have to stress that, we have doubts as to whether the penalties proposed are acceptable in the end. The position of the club of the Polish People's Party will be expressed in the vote. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u40	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Public Finance Commission of 7 February 2017 on the Government's draft law on the exchange of tax information with other countries, originally in print No 1091. During the permanent subcommittee meeting to monitor the tax system on 11 January 2017 we introduced a series of legislative and regulatory amendments to the original bill, which strengthened the readability and precision of the proposed solutions in the government project, which implements several Council directives into the Polish legal order and amends six tax laws. The government draft law on the exchange of tax information with other countries sets out the principles, modalities and characteristics of the authorities for the exchange of tax information with other countries. This draft also sets out the obligations of financial institutions for the exchange of tax information and for the automatic exchange of tax information on Reportable Accounts and for the control of their execution. It introduces regulations allowing automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation with non-EU countries. The need for mutual assistance between different tax administrations is becoming increasingly important. The mobility of taxpayers and the internationalisation of financial instruments are constantly increasing, while the process of determining the amount of taxes due is becoming increasingly difficult. Undisclosed and untaxed income significantly reduces national tax revenues. Action is therefore needed to improve the effectiveness of the fight against tax fraud and tax evasion by strongly promoting the automatic exchange of information as a future European international standard for the transparency of information exchange in tax matters. Without mutual cooperation between tax administrations, a single Member State cannot effectively combat tax fraud and tax evasion. In order to overcome the negative effects of these phenomena, it is necessary to create an appropriate system of cooperation between tax administrations. Effective administrative cooperation between Member States is needed under conditions consistent with the proper functioning of the internal market by strengthening the tools and mechanisms for the exchange of information. It is common practice in the European Union to issue interpretations of individual tax laws that facilitate consistent and transparent application of the law. The practice of explaining tax law to taxpayers allows businesses to operate under more certainty, which may encourage them to invest and comply with the law. The proposed rules also aim to strengthen activities aimed at under-taxation and income transfer between subsidiaries and the parent undertaking in the case of large-scale companies - this is a condition for exceeding EUR 750 million. Unlike small and medium-sized enterprises, the so-called. MNE groups have sufficient resources to exploit gaps in national law or international tax law to shift profits between countries in order to reduce the tax paid. The tax authorities therefore need comprehensive and reliable data on the structure, transfer pricing policy and transactions between related parties. The purpose of the amendment is therefore to introduce appropriate transparency requirements for the activities of the entities forming part of the group of entities. Greater transparency should encourage these groups of actors to reduce inappropriate practices. The scope of the reporting procedures depends on whether the account is a high-value account, since it is advisable to significantly reduce tax fraud, especially international taxpayers trading huge amounts. During the work of the Sejm in the Permanent Sub-Committee to monitor the tax system [[♪ The bell ♪]] and during the meeting of the Finance Committee on 7 February 2017, as a result of a broad discussion in this draft law, we have introduced the right, necessary legislative, linguistic and precise responsibilities of all parties, and we have defined the time for the entry into force of this draft. The very important amendment we have introduced is to adapt the bill to the structure of the National Tax Administration. To sum up...

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u44	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Gentlemen of Ministers! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law on the exchange of tax information with other countries, print no. 1091. The aim of the bill is to implement four directives of the Council of the European Union concerning the introduction of mandatory automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation into the Polish legal order. Directive 2014/107 of the Council of the European Union of 9 December 2014 introducing European and international standards for transparency and exchange of information in tax matters is essential. In addition, the Act introduces regulations enabling the automatic exchange of tax information with countries other than the EU based on the previous agreement of the Republic of Poland on this matter. The need for these regulations is due to a dynamic increase in business mobility and the resulting surge in cross-border transactions. The lack of flow of information for tax purposes seriously makes it difficult for tax authorities to correctly determine the tax bases and then to determine the amount of tax liability due. The problems of tax authorities in obtaining the necessary tax information are often used by taxpayers to tax evasion and even tax fraud. The lack of close cooperation between tax administrations in individual countries makes it ineffective and, more importantly, from one year to the next, these phenomena are becoming more and more widespread. The creation of a coherent system of cooperation between tax authorities in individual countries is therefore not only necessary but also very urgent. The EU directives aim to implement mechanisms to prevent tax avoidance and fraud, thereby increasing the efficiency and efficiency of tax collection. It is appropriate to agree that such significant and extensive legislation, in particular the implementation of EU law, is in favour of regulating the exchange of tax information in a single legal act. Section III, in detail, is essential for the automatic exchange of information on reported accounts. The reporting financial institution shall be required to apply due diligence procedures and specific reporting procedures, as well as to record the activities undertaken and to collect the required documentation. Reporting obligations include reporting accounts and undocumented accounts. The Act provides for exemption from the obligation to report accounts fulfilling the strict conditions set out in the Act. The Reporting Financial Institution shall provide information on reported accounts and undocumented accounts by means of electronic communication for the period of a calendar year. The draft law sets out due diligence procedures for existing individual accounts and entities' accounts, including new entity accounts, and regulates in detail the procedures for the control of financial institutions, as well as the amount of fines for failure to comply with statutory obligations. The draft law sets out detailed arrangements for the automatic exchange of tax information on entities forming part of a group of entities understood as a capital group. Such situations will certainly occur more and more in the economic space. The date of entry into force of the Act woke up, and I think it still raises serious doubts. There are problems even in the case of clearing the costs of cases raised by Poles before the European Court of Human Rights. Therefore, the draft Public Finance Act introduces changes to complement, clarify and organise existing legal regulations both in respect of civil and non-tax public-law claims. The draft establishes that these claims may be remitted either ex officio or on request, taking into account the general clauses, inter alia. the important interest of the debtor or of the public interest, regulates the possibility of non-payment of civil claims from local government units and their units in amounts not exceeding PLN 100. The report shall then expire on 30 November of the year following the financial year. The next amendment refers to the rationalisation of the rules for the establishment by the Minister responsible for social security of a list of public benefit organisations. Another amendment concerns the extension of the source catalogue of revenue of the Public Benefit Support Fund by interest from the so-called "public benefit organisation." free resources transferred to the management of the Minister responsible for public finances. At a committee meeting on 24 January, the draft law was adopted unanimously, without amendment. The Commission recommends that the High House adopt this draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The current strict rules for the inclusion of public benefit organisations in the list entitled to receive 1% are relaxed. tax. In accordance with the proposed arrangements, the Minister will also include in the list those organisations which will prove that the failure to include the required annual accounts: financial and substantive has occurred without their fault or for reasons beyond their control. The Government also amends other provisions on compliance with the reporting obligation to the Minister. So far, the lack of precision in the time limits required for reporting has raised considerable legal concerns among the organisations. In addition, the rules governing reporting obligations for organisations which have lost their status as a public benefit organisation are simplified. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u84	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of my club, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, in relation to the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Senate bill amending the Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and some other laws, print No. 778, report - print No. 1226. The proposed regulation provides that the repeal or amendment of the final decision of the pension authority will in principle not be possible after the end of: 10 years, where: the decision was given as a result of the offence, the evidence on the basis of which the facts relevant to the case were found to be false, the decision was taken as a result of the deliberate misappropriation of the pension authority by the person receiving the benefit; 5 years, where: after the decision has been enforced, new evidence is submitted or new facts have been disclosed before the decision has been issued which affect the right to benefits or their amount, the decision has been taken on the basis of a different decision or judgment of the court which was subsequently repealed or annulled. It will not be possible to issue them after 3 years where the granting of benefits or incorrect calculation of their amount was due to an error of the pension authority. However, the time limits introduced will not apply if, as a result of the repeal or amendment of the decision, the person concerned acquires a higher right to benefit or benefit or if the decision to fix the initial capital which has not been taken into account for the calculation of the pension as determined by the final decision is subject to the repeal or amendment. According to Art. 1g in Article 114 of the pension law, the pension authority will waive the repeal or amendment of a decision where the granting of benefits or the incorrect calculation of benefits was due to an error of the pension authority, where the revocation or amendment of the decision would entail an excessive burden on the person concerned due to his or her personal or material situation, age, health status or other specific circumstances. This draft also includes provisions making similar changes to the Act on pension provision for professional soldiers and their families, as well as the Act on pension provision for police officers, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, Central Anti-corruption Office, Border Guard, Government Protection Office, State Fire Department and Prison Service and their families. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u85	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Mrs Joanna Frydrych, the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u86	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the draft law amending the Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and certain other laws contained in print No 778. The draft law amending the Social Insurance Fund Act and certain other laws aims to take account of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 February 2012. It should be recalled that the Law of 20 April 2004 amending the Law on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and certain other laws, with effect from 1 July 2004, the legislator in Article. The Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund introduced paragraph 114. 1a, who said that the right to benefits or their amount shall be re-established at the request of the person concerned or of his own motion if, after the decision on benefits has been passed, new evidence has been submitted or circumstances have been revealed before the decision has been issued which affect the right to benefits or their amount and if, after the decision has been adopted, it appears that the evidence submitted did not give rise to the determination of the right to or the amount of the pension. This meant that the issuing authority could verify the correctness of the decision, the final decision at any time and because it had assessed the evidence previously submitted differently or otherwise classified those facts which it had previously treated as justifying the granting of certain benefits. The Constitutional Tribunal held, in its judgment of 28 February 2012, that Article. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a The Social Insurance Fund pension law is incompatible with the principle of citizens' confidence in the State and with the law that they have established under Article. Article 67 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. As a result of the delivery of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal as from 8 March 2012, Art. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a The Social Insurance Fund pension law has ceased to apply. This means that the right to benefits or their amount shall be re-established at the request of the person concerned or of his own motion if, after the decision on benefits has been passed, new evidence is submitted or circumstances have been revealed before that decision has been issued which affect the right to benefits or their amount. This draft law amends fully taking into account the guidance contained in the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, giving the pension authority the opportunity to repeal or amend the final decision if the period has not expired since its date of issue: 10 years, where: the decision was given as a result of the offence, the evidence on the basis of which the relevant facts were established proved false and the decision was given as a result of the deliberate misappropriation of the pension authority by the person receiving the benefit; 5 years where, after the decision has become final, new evidence is submitted or new facts have been disclosed before the decision has been issued which affect the right to benefits or their amount, and the decision has been based on another decision or decision which was subsequently repealed or annulled, and 3 years where the granting of benefits or incorrect calculation of their amount has occurred as a result of an error of the pension authority. The proposed changes are very necessary as they specify specific deadlines and a specific catalogue of circumstances empowering the pension authorities to move their decisions to their own level. The proposed amendments ensure a balance between the interests of the individual and the public interest and take into account the principle of protection of confidence in the State and its rights as laid down by the Constitutional Tribunal. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club The Civic Platform supports the proposed bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u88	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Until recently, until 2012, the legal status allowed for an unlimited change in decisions on pensions. The verification was not only permitted due to the disclosure of new evidence and circumstances, since only recently, under Polish law, a ZUS official could legally decide to determine the right to retirement or pension change from office at any time to the detriment of pensioners, even in cases where the previous decision was solely the result of an error of the official. Even if, after 10 years of payment to a pensioner or pensionist, the ZUS official only verified his own assessment of the evidence, he could have a final decision, I stress, the decision on the basis of which for many years the citizen received a payment of a certain amount, move and reduce the amount of the benefit - unlimited authority in the hands of the officials of the ZUS, all in accordance with the applicable law at that time. This state of law violated the fundamental constitutional principles of our system: the principle of citizen's confidence in the state, the principle of protection of acquired rights and gave too broad powers to administrative authorities. The Constitutional Tribunal ruled in its judgment of 28 February 2012, eliminating the provision of a trap from the legal system. There's no way to disagree with this diagnosis. Of course, verification of the decision should be possible, but in very specific cases and based on precise conditions. The Senate draft law takes into account the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal and clarifies such conditions, allowing for a change of decision in certain cases and within a certain period of time. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u90	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate draft law amending the Social Insurance Fund Act and certain other laws follows the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 February 2012 declaring the non-compliance of the article. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a the Act of 17 December 1998 on pensions from the Social Insurance Fund with a Constitution. The contested article. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a provided that the provision of the Article. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Pension Act shall apply mutatis mutandis if, after the decision has been passed, it appears that the evidence submitted did not give rise to the determination of the right to a pension or to the amount of the pension. In practice, such an understanding of the provision has been established that the authority has differently assessed the evidence previously submitted or classified those facts which it had previously regarded as justifying the granting of certain benefits. This led to a breach of the legal security of the entity. In this case, the legitimate decisions could have been verified ex officio to the detriment of the recipient, at any time, indefinitely, and even if the error of the decision was due to the error of the pension authority, which was otherwise obliged to examine the grounds for acquiring the right to the benefit in question. Period of collection of the benefit received in accordance with Article. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a was not treated as a contribution or as a non-contributory, which could, as a result, constitute an obstacle to the acquisition of the right to other social security benefits, e.g. pensions for incapacity for work. The re-fixing of the right to benefits must be justified by exceptional circumstances in order to maintain a balance between the legitimate interests of the entity and the public interest. The Act introduces a new wording of the provisions on the mobility of established social benefits. The method used is the calculation of expressis verbis of the grounds for repealing or amending the decision. Those conditions are: the submission of new evidence or the disclosure of new circumstances existing before decisions are taken which affect the right to benefits or their amount, situations where the decision was taken as a result of a crime or evidence on the basis of which it was established that the facts relevant to the case would prove false, it will be established that the decision was taken as a result of an informed error of the pension authority by the person receiving the benefit or that the decision was taken on the basis of another decision or decision of the court which was subsequently repealed, amended or annulled. On the basis of the grounds relating to the need to protect the public interest, which is clearly in favour of the proper disposal of the Social Insurance Fund, where it is found that the wrong granting or the determination of the benefit is the responsibility of the pension authority, this is only permissible within 3 years of the date of the decision. The occurrence of any of the grounds for repealing or amending the decision will only enable the pension authority to issue a decision which is unfavourable to the recipient within a period of 10 or 5 years. The party shall be notified of the initiation of an ex officio procedure by the beneficiary body. An additional security for the recipient is the establishment of a negative indication which does not allow the decision of the pension authority to be repealed or amended in the event of a significant deterioration in the personal or material situation of the person concerned due to his age, health or other specific circumstances. The Kukiz Club15 supports this project. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u93	Thank you very much, Member. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy, Minister Marcin Zielenecki, will answer the question. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u94	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer your question, I will refer to the history of the Senate draft law that has been debated today amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law. I would like to point out that this is a project which is the result of the Constitutional Tribunal's ruling in 2012 on the non-compliance of the article. 114 Act on pensions from the Social Insurance Fund with a constitution, with the principle of citizens' trust in the state. It can be said that the Senate is the authority of the State which is responsible for the implementation of decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Constitutional Tribunal issued the so-called. Scope ruling, i.e. considers that this provision, in so far as it can be said, gives unlimited time for the possibility of touching the final decisions of pension bodies, simply violates the standards of the Constitution. The result of this ruling was a change in the practice of the Social Insurance Institution. As early as 2012, the guidelines of the President of the Social Insurance Institution were issued, which could be said to have changed the rules of procedure of the Social Insurance Institution in cases where irregularities previously issued by the pension authority were found, both in terms of determining the right to benefit and in terms of determining the amount of that benefit. These guidelines were intended to introduce - I would say - an instructional but quite important period from the point of view of the practice of the Social Insurance Institution, a 5-year period. With regard to decisions issued after 5 years, or the possibility of moving final decisions after 5 years of the decision's validity, this simply became impossible according to these guidelines and the Social Security Office - it can be said - followed these solutions in practice. On the other hand, it has become necessary to put the legal situation in order because, from the point of view of the legal provisions in force, formally, the provisions of the Article. 114 have not been changed. This proposal also provides, first, for the detailed implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2012, but also for both the correction of the practice applied so far on the basis of the guidelines by the Social Insurance Institution and the substantive amendment of the provisions consisting in varying the length of the period after which the final decision of the pension authority could be affected depending on the circumstances of such incorrect decision, depending on whether this decision is the result of the error of the pension body itself or of certain circumstances which sometimes burden even the insured person himself. I also want to say that this project also includes... In other words, it makes the practice of the Social Insurance Institution more flexible because it gives the Social Insurance Institution the opportunity to withdraw from such a final decision of the pension authority when the repeal or amendment of a previous decision - the final decision - would entail an excessive burden on the person concerned because of his or her personal or material situation, age, health status or other specific circumstances. So that this element included in the project was positively assessed in the official position of the Council of Ministers. We believe that this individual approach to the personal, material, health situation of each insured person affected by such an incorrect decision of the pension authority is a positive solution here. For this reason, it turned out that the mere change in the practice of the Social Insurance Institution based on instructional solutions, the guidelines of the President of the Social Insurance Institution are insufficient. We therefore welcome the amended draft law on amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, which has been modified during parliamentary work, including in committee. I ask the High House to support this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u98	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The amendments proposed by the applicants meet the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. It's about art. Article 114 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a of the 1998 Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, which allows the Social Insurance Fund to reduce and even deprive the pension if, after the decision establishing the right to it has been passed, it appears that the evidence on which the decision was based did not give rise to the granting of a pension or pension. The Court did not, in principle, contest the need to verify final pension decisions. On the other hand, it found it incompatible with the Constitution that the pension authority had the power to initiate ex officio proceedings in order to verify the established pension rights or the amount of the pension. The judges indicated in the statement of reasons that the Social Security Office as a specialised public body should carry out its social security audit tasks without undue delay. It has the right tools to achieve this objective. At the same time, it was considered that the Social Security Office was not too eager to enforce its debts, although on the other hand it was always very meticulous. Reason? Although the principal amount was symbolic, interest was no longer. Under the amendments, the Social Security Office will be able to decide to resume the procedure for granting a pension and the amount of those benefits in the event of tampering with evidence, knowingly misleading or decision-making as a result of a crime, within 10 years of the decision being taken, as well as in case of disclosure of new circumstances or evidence unknown to the body on the date of the decision, within 5 years of the decision. Re-decisions on pensions or other benefits may be given in the event of revocation, amendment or annulment of the decision of the court on the basis of which the decision to grant the benefit was taken, within 5 years of the decision being taken, as well as in the event of a benefit being awarded or miscalculated as a result of an error by the determining authority, within 3 years of the decision being taken. In practice, the proposed amendment will also mean the protection of the certainty and stability of the legal order and the exclusion of claims from the pension body, which would be separated from the actual situation of the insured person in view of the significant expiry of the period from the date on which they were created at the time of their completion. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u138	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded health care. Today's amendment is the result of an analysis of the functioning of the so-called. the oncology package and as part of its rapid oncology therapy, a solution that allows patients with malignant cancer to gain a real chance of recovery by intensifying their actions leading to diagnosis and subsequent treatment. The government's proposal proposes a number of changes that will significantly improve the effectiveness of this solution. These include simplifying reporting of waiting lists and simplifying the diagnostics and oncology treatment card. It is planned to extend the list of doctors who will be able to issue a DiLO card, which is beneficial and will significantly facilitate the work and speed up the diagnostic process of patients for whom the detection of the disease at the earliest stage is a key factor in recovery. In my view, the new powers given to the Health Minister will also serve to increase the efficiency and availability of the benefits in question in the future. In addition to the favourable changes proposed by the applicants, there are also those which raise my concerns and concerns. The first is to give up the cancer recognition rate, after which the primary healthcare professional had to undergo training in cancer recognition in order to regain the right to issue a DiLO card. While I consider the temporary withdrawal of the possibility of issuing a DiLO card to be a disadvantage for patients, the obligation of a specialist doctor to provide training was only to increase his knowledge and to improve his work efficiency and consequently to serve patients. I also share the doubts raised during the meeting of the Committee on Health regarding the possibility of withdrawing from consilies. I am afraid that the wording contained in the impact assessment for the draft regulation of the Minister of Health that talks about planning the introduction of this possibility may have very far-reaching negative effects on patients. The absence of a specific list of cases where this withdrawal is medically justified may have counterproductive effects. The mere imposition of an obligation to include justification in the card is not a preventive action, especially since the control may take place after injury has been caused to the patient. I hope that my doubts will be resolved during the further procession. At the same time, the multitude of positive aspects of the project lead to its support.

Consequently, that provision is incompatible with the principle that the regulations are issued by the bodies indicated in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland on the basis of the detailed authorisation provided for in the Act and for the purpose of its implementation. This project eliminates the identified non-compliances and introduces new solutions, some of which need to be recalled here. The current Regulation lists all its members by name. In line with the new solution, there will be only a number of members in the Regulation, which will eliminate the current need for legislative amendments for any personal change. To date, the chairman and members of the committee have been appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration. Now, the powers to appoint members will lie solely in the hands of the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. public administration. In addition, the project sets out requirements for potential candidates. These include legal education, at least a 5-year period of employment in the public finance sector, etc., although it seems reasonable to change the content of the article. Point 2 of Article 1 Article 18a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 4 point 4 from the very discretionary and unclear draft in defining the condition: gives the guarantee of proper implementation of the tasks of the committee for the condition proposed in the 3rd minority proposal: it was not convicted by a final judgment for intentional crimes, which seems to be on the spot here. The project further sets out the modalities for financing the National Court of Justice, introduces a legislative delegation to issue a regulation by the Minister responsible for Justice. public administration on the level of remuneration of members of the board of directors - the possibility of reducing remuneration in certain cases. Four minority proposals were submitted to the project, which, as amendments tabled at the committee meeting, did not receive approval from the committee, and the Committee on Local Government and Regional Policy, after considering this draft law at its meeting on 26 January 2017, asks the Sejm to adopt this law without amendment. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u178	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour, on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Infrastructure, which was sitting yesterday at the joint meeting, to submit a report on the draft law on the monitoring system for road freight transport submitted to the High House in print No 1244. It's a government project. Its rules are designed to protect legitimate trade in goods such as goods. motor fuels and derivatives including biodiesel, fuel additives, lubricating oils, vegetable oils to be used as additives for motor fuels. This law also applies to goods such as antifreezes based on ethyl alcohol, thinners and solvents, partially and completely contaminated ethyl alcohol and tobacco droughts. So far, the largest grey zone and most tax irregularities have been identified in trade in these goods. In a report prepared in 2015 to the European Commission, the then government of the Civic Platform and PSL stated that the tax gap in this tax, in VAT, increased to 27%. The tax irregularities that occurred over the years consisted in the levying and non-payment of taxes on goods and services and the reduction of excise duties. In the liquid fuel market, VAT and excise reduction amounted to PLN 6033 million in 2015, and in 2014 these reductions amounted to PLN 2085 million. This shows how fast these abnormalities grew. The effect of this practice over the years is not only to reduce tax revenue to the State budget, but also to violate the principle of fair competition and to burden honest taxpayers. It should be pointed out that the most common illicit trade in goods concerns trade in goods of unknown origin. On many occasions, I paid attention to the functioning on the Polish market of an even primitive system for the disinfection of ethyl alcohol and disregarding the hygiene rules for placing it on the market. All the comments and the analyses that were made were really untraceable in the laws. The application of the scheme continued, effectively reducing revenue in the budget and affecting the balance of fair market players and corruption. The government project we are debating today introduces regulations that seal the tax system and introduces a system for monitoring and controlling the carriage of goods by road, which will eliminate fraud of undue returns only on the basis of falsified invoices. The project also introduces the obligation to report goods to the register, with this register being kept in the electronic system and the administrator of this system being the head of the National Tax Administration. The obligation to report sensitive goods to the register shall be imposed on consignors and consignees and on carriers supplying goods to the country and carrying goods in transit through our country. This element of the details introduced has led to a discussion during the committee's work, as it was not clear to all Members why carriers would also be included in this register. After clarification by the Minister, the provisions proposed in this regard by the Government were maintained. At the same time, failing to register or incorrectly comply with it will result in a penalty of money or a fine. I would like to point out that, in order to determine the possibility for the Minister of Finance to issue a regulation, the Minister, who, as a result of market analysis and monitoring, finds that there are negative trends around other goods, today not included as sensitive goods, will be able, at the request of the carrier or supplier, to consider whether the recipient may waive or reduce the penalty imposed. The system proposed by the Act is therefore a flexible system. At the same time, it creates the possibility for the competent minister to react quickly to what is happening on the market and to catch new goods which are affected by irregularities. I will mention that when we introduced the reverse VAT system into scrap metal, then illegal sales of telephones appeared very quickly on the market. The market is very mobile, which is why this tool in the Minister's hands is a very important tool. I think it will be used effectively. The provisions of the Act define precisely the obligations and deadlines of the parties involved in the trading venue for sensitive goods. High Izbo! Finally, I want to return briefly to the anticipated penalties. All participants in the supply of sensitive goods were subject to sanctions. The proposed system of penalties is transparent and aims at preventive action. Hence the penalties in relation to the applicable ones are high. The project offers the possibility of imposing a fine of up to 46%, i.e. twice the current VAT rate. For example, for those quantities which have not been declared, i.e. the difference between the quantity of goods declared and actually transported, the authorities will be able to levy 46 per cent tax and at the same time the penalty must not be less than 20 000. zł. This has also triggered discussion in committees, but Members have agreed that this system must be a system that strongly warns that it will not pay to conduct criminal proceedings. However, the law gives the possibility to appeal against the penalty imposed and gives the determining authority the opportunity to verify the amount of the penalty imposed. For example, when the carrier fails to complete the application for new goods, it is also expected that 5 000 will be punished. zł. The legislator has also defined a catalogue of entities exempt from the obligations under the Act. High Izbo! The Act shall enter into force on 1 March 2017. On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Infrastructure, I ask the High Chamber to adopt a law on the system for monitoring the carriage of goods by road in the version submitted in the report of both committees in print No 1269. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u218	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The payment of alimony is an undisputed duty of those obliged to children or to adults, because it can act both ways, especially when this obligation arises from a judicial decision, among other things, but not only. Unfortunately, in Polish reality, this obligation is ignored. We have therefore analysed the data from which about 1 million children do not receive alimony. The effectiveness of chamber executions is at one of the lowest levels in the European Union, at 19,5% , when e.g. Denmark is 88%. In turn, the effectiveness of the maintenance fund, which should carry out these tasks, is only 6.04% to maintenance debtors. The economic information offices registered 276 thousand. maintenance debtors and their total debt amounts to almost PLN 9 billion. These data must shock and therefore our change is necessary. At the same time, when we look at solutions in other legal systems, there are no restrictions that still exist in the enforcement of money due to entitled persons, in particular children. So that the aim of the project is absolutely social and serves to realise the idea of social justice, but also the principle of an appropriate criminal response, to prosecute those who are obliged to pay alimony, and yet do not pay. Why aren't they paying? Due to the fact that we have two criteria in the bill that effectively enable the perpetrators of these crimes to escape into impunity. Namely, the first of these criteria concerns persistence - the criterion of persistence, which is interpreted differently in case law, interpreted differently by the courts and which results in a significant number of cases ending in remission. In recent years, 2010.2015, on average 21% cases ended by referring an indictment to the court, 24% of the proceedings were dismissed and 42% refused to initiate proceedings. There are situations where the perpetrator pays a symbolic amount, then refuses to pay, again after 2, after 3 months he returns to pay only a fraction of that amount and this prevents him from being effectively prosecuted. Therefore, we eliminate the mark in the form of persistent avoidance from maintenance obligations. Finally, we eliminate the second impact mark in the form of exposure of the person entitled to the inability to meet the basic needs of life. We introduce an objective criterion in the form of maintenance arrears equivalent to at least three periodic benefits or a delay in the outstanding benefit other than that of at least 3 months. At the same time, we leave the mark of evasion. This is necessary, so that, in colloquially speaking, people do not fall into the trap of the justice system who, for objective reasons, are unable to pay, e.g. they cannot take up employment despite their efforts or their health makes it impossible for them to take up work. That's why we leave this safety valve in the form of a sign that's going away. At the same time, greater responsibility will be placed on the part of those who, failing to comply with the maintenance obligation, expose the person entitled to the inability to meet the basic needs of life. In other words, what is the basic type today becomes a qualified type, and in this basic one we introduce an objective criterion. Two circumstances are aimed at extracting the perpetrator, excluding liability, namely a situation in which the perpetrator will eliminate the effects of his criminal behaviour and pay maintenance arrears no later than 30 days from the date of his first hearing - he will therefore be able to count on the failure to comply with the penalty and to waive the penalty. Therefore, these solutions will certainly contribute to the fuller maintenance security of eligible persons and the rules that are currently a paravan for criminals will become a viable means of prosecuting these crimes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Attention should be paid to the possibility of changing the criterion for courts to determine the amount of alimony, for example by objecting to it, in order to rule out to debtors an argument of too high alimony which is impossible for them to pay. That's where this government project came from, which is supposed to seal our system, eliminate some of the ambiguities that we've seen in the system. Today, I want to tell you that, as regards the limitation of offences, there will be no longer, I hope, the situation with which we met in the case of Frog: 81 statute-of-law offences and games with the police, which have excited and irritated the whole society at the same time. Those offences have been statute of limitations, for a period of two years since the act was committed is often too short for the court to settle the case. A well-prepared lawyer is able, using certain tricks, to maximize this time and the court cannot fit in these 2 years. The case is often opened as a criminal case, which means there is a completely different qualification. Time, of course, runs, and then there is a change of this qualification, we are dealing with an offence, time is running out, and it is not the responsibility of the person who should bear this responsibility. This is the first thing: not 2 years and 3 years. Of course, we leave this one-year period since the act was committed, during which the case must be initiated, but the two-year period since the act was committed is too short. A completely new deadline is introduced: 3 years. The second point is that people in a state of drunkenness cause accidents that result in death or severe injury to the person's health. Today, according to the Penal Code, such a person is responsible for 9 months to 12 years. 9 months, which means that a conditional suspension of the penalty may be applied. That's not gonna happen anymore. People who intoxicate, kill a person or are drugged or drugged or drugged, are to be put behind bars. Therefore, we raise the lower statutory penalty, which will be 2 years. The upper one is left unchanged, so 2 years to 12 years, which means that the execution of the sentence will not be conditionally suspended. The third issue is the emergence of a completely new type of prohibited act - this is a matter of escape from the police. This is a situation that, unfortunately, often happens, a situation that is very dangerous, which is that we are dealing with a person who does not stop for police control, but runs away from the police. Light signals, sound signals are on, and we have no doubt that we are dealing not with accidental failure, which happens in life, can happen, for example due to weather conditions, but with the escape of a person. There is a chase, often this person enters the city, causing a real, very big threat to the safety of people. And I want to tell you that today the police are arresting this person, and the driver is most often responsible only for the offence. This is a misdemeanor. This can't be right. This is actually a very big danger for people who move, whether in road traffic or even for people who live in cities, because this is most often the case in cities. We change these rules here, it will no longer be a criminal offence, it will be a criminal offence, which will be punishable by imprisonment up to the age of 5 years, and we believe that this is - of course I am talking about this upper statutory threat - a penalty appropriate to the degree of threat to this type of criminal offence. Another case concerns what is actually on the table in front of a judge ruling on a particular case when he decides on a road safety crime case. Very often there is no complete picture of the person who is facing this court, most often only has an extract from the KRK register - the National Criminal Register. This is a problem because there are not revealed all, to say the least, traffic offences, road piracy, any violations, offences or crimes. KRK is not enough, there must be a new system. This new system will be based on the fact that we involve the court in the obligation to obtain the data from the records that the police have in their resources, i.e. the records of drivers, the register that pictures, for example. Penalty points, how many criminal points a person has in his account, but also from the second record, the Central Registration of Vehicles and Drivers, from which we can find out, for example, whether decisions have been taken to withdraw driving rights in a given case. Only then, if we have information from these two records, as well as from the KRK, will the court have a full picture of who it is really dealing with, and will it be able to actually give a judgment just to the person concerned. Another issue is the court's ruling on the necessity of a mandatory criminal measure - a ban on driving. This prohibition, let me remind you, may be imposed as a criminal measure in the range from one year to 15 years. We have a curial situation - art. 180a. We have a situation in which the administrative authority has decided to withdraw powers. This decision is being violated. Someone can't drive the car, but they get in the car and get caught by the police. And imagine that, in 2015, in fact, 1,400 such cases, the court, although it could have ruled as a criminal measure mandatory to take away the right to drive, only in four cases ruled that criminal measure. This is an unbelievable thing. We introduce the mandatory release of this criminal measure, the application of this criminal measure, the compulsory withdrawal of powers not only in this case, but also in the second case where there is a real pursuit of the person concerned. Just like you. I told you, it won't be a misdemeanor anymore, but it will be a crime, and it will also be criminalized. This is a very important matter. At the same time, as far as the border is concerned, we raise to 5 years the responsibility for failing to comply with the criminal justice measure. Today it's a punishment of 3 years, should be 5 years. In today's legal situation, in case of a breach of the administrative ban, it is 2 years, a judicial remedy - 3 years. This is too small a difference between breaking the administrative ban and a court-martial ban as a criminal measure. Therefore, I am presenting a government project, asking the High House to be kind to lean on it, to support it.
