ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u278	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present on behalf of the applicants a draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act contained in print No 1166. Ladies and gentlemen! This law in its construction is very simple and readable. We want to amend two existing articles of the Code of Criminal Procedure, Art. 209 and Art. 210. Both relate to the rather difficult responsibilities of prosecutors in matters relating to the opening of the corpse in order to perform the autopsy and excavation of the grave, so-called. Exhumation. A few months ago this topic became a very, very hot topic and was the subject of a very lively and hot, difficult discussion for many people. In this bill, we propose that the prosecutor who conducts the investigation may withdraw from the examination of the corpse when he considers that due to his investigation and other reasons which appear in this investigation, there is no need for a postmortem. The second article deals with a very, very important issue of the possibility of a complaint against the prosecutor's decision concerning the so-called. Exhumation. The right to challenge any decision is one of the constitutional standards and it is strange that, in the case of an exhumation decision, the decision of the prosecution cannot be challenged in its current form. This proposal for an amendment was, of course, created in a very specific situation. We describe it in the explanatory memorandum. Namely, the issue of ordered exhumations of victims of the Smolensk catastrophe. The context is that many families of victims of the Smolensk disaster do not agree with the arbitrary decision of the prosecutor's office, have tried to complain about this decision by way of existing law, unfortunately such a complaint in the current legal situation, as I said, does not exist. It seems strange because the possibility of a complaint should exist. The decision to exhume is a decision very strongly interfering with the feelings of the loved ones, it is a madly traumatic decision for the family of the victim of any catastrophe, certainly also for the families of the victims of the Smolensk catastrophe. It seems reasonable to be able to challenge this arbitrary decision of the prosecution simply to exist. Of course, each of these complaints would be dealt with by the court, but this change would be a way out, an attempt to understand the feelings and emotions of those families of the victims of the Smolensk disaster, which simply did not want to accept exhumations in the case of their loved ones. When we listened to many of the prosecutors currently investigating the Smolensk disaster, there were such opinions that unfortunately these exhumations could not be waived. This is, of course, a question of interpretation - whether they can or can't - because different voices fell on the subject. But it was certainly said by the prosecutors: this is a legal obligation, we have to do it and there is no possibility to challenge this decision, well, we would even like to feel in the situation of families of victims of the Smolensk catastrophe who do not agree to exhumation, but we cannot. This decision is uncomplainable. All the more reason for this bill to go against what the prosecutors said. If such a decision has been taken, it should be possible to appeal it to the decision of the court. I do not want to introduce too much politics here, although we all know that the Smolensk disaster is what separates us very deeply: it divides the political scene and also divides Poles. A lot of politics, a lot of bad emotions came in here, but certainly the arbitrary decision of the prosecution to exhume the bodies of all the victims of the Smolensk catastrophe did not cool off these emotions. She rather warmed up these emotions and made this dispute even hotter, because she interfered in such a painful sphere of life for families. All the more so, it seems that we should apply this constitutional standard in this respect to the fact that it should be possible to appeal from each decision, especially since, as I said, at this stage it is an arbitrary decision of the prosecution. I refer to the issue of families of victims of the Smolensk catastrophe, to what is happening with the Smolensk exhumations, because on the basis of this we have submitted this bill, but it seems that the problem is much, much deeper and that the possibility of bringing this complaint does not exist is an obvious oversight in Polish law. Polish constitutional protection, legal protection is subject to respect and honor for the dead, also subject to feelings of loved ones, and in this context this challenge seems even more legitimate. I hope that, above the divisions in the case that differ us in the issue of the Smolensk disaster, we will also be able to look at some of the families of the victims of the Smolensk disaster, which simply do not agree to these exhumations. These people should also be deeply respected and spared, if they do not wish, the trauma of what happened in Smolensk. That's why it's a bill. I hope it will be widely supported by parliamentary clubs. In this matter, let us really run away from current politics, let us try to listen to the voices of those who have been very experienced by the Smolensk disaster, who have lost loved ones in it, who do not want to experience this trauma again. They should also have the opportunity to comment on this matter and should also be able to appeal against the arbitrary decision of the prosecution. I am, of course, at your disposal if there are any questions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u284	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Well, as has been pointed out, in the current legal state, the examination and opening of the corpse is carried out when there is a suspicion of a criminal death. Importantly, these actions shall be undertaken in a mandatory manner when the prosecutor unilaterally deems it appropriate. I would like to make it clear here that applicants do not question this means of proof in the draft law and do not intend to delete it from the Penal Code. The proposed amendment aims only to regulate the control of the use of this evidence. The regulation in its current legal state raises a number of legitimate objections. It should therefore be considered appropriate to amend the Article. 209 Code of Criminal Procedure by the addition of § 6. As indicated by the applicant's representative, there was no clear legal basis to withdraw from the examination when the circumstances had changed since the decision to carry out these investigations had been taken. This is an important proposal from the point of view of the practical treatment of pre-trial or judicial proceedings. If, after carrying out other evidence activities, the issue of criminal manslaughter is clarified, it is possible to refrain from carrying out an examination. We are all aware that these activities are always difficult for families and those close to the victim and cause many emotions. The amendment to the article seems to be more relevant. 210 Code of Criminal Procedure. It introduces a review of the decision to carry out an inspection of the corpse. In the current state of law, these quite difficult for loved ones, as I have already mentioned, are not subject to any control. This means that even legitimate arguments, whether those who are close to you or others, will not matter if the prosecutor does not share them. This often leads to many dramatic situations that could simply be avoided. The applicant therefore introduces the possibility to file a complaint against the order of the prosecutor or the court in this regard. This is part of the constitutional principles of the right to challenge decisions as well as the protection of private life. There is no doubt that the activities related to the examination of the corpse affect the private life of people close to the victim, and in particular the family. In this respect, the State should provide legal tools to guarantee citizens the protection of their constitutional rights and freedoms. Therefore, the Civic Platform Club welcomes the proposed amendments to the Code of Criminal Procedure and requests that the printing be sent to the committee for further work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u292	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I would like to present our position on the Parliamentary draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure in print No 1166. The aim of this project is to protect the interests of those who oppose the autopsy of their loved ones and to protect the worship of the dead. The authors of the project write in the explanatory memorandum that it is about the interests of the families of the victims of the Smolensk catastrophe. The proposed changes are intended to enable families of the dead to take legal action, who refuse to exhume their loved ones. In accordance with the current rules in the event of death as a result of the crime, an examination and opening of the corpse shall be carried out. Depending on the stage of the proceedings, either the court or the prosecutor shall carry out such inspections. In the current state of law, in order to inspect or open a dead body, either the prosecutor or the court may order the removal of the dead from the grave by proceedings. That provision shall not be subject to any appeal. The aim of this project is to add to the article. 209 of the Law of 6 June 1977. The Code of Criminal Procedure allows the prosecutor to waive the opening of a corpse in the event that he considers that such evidence is no longer justified in the course of the investigation and to provide for the possibility of appeal against the provisions of the bodies referred to in Article. 210 k.p.k. Implementation of this amendment to Article. 210 will provide an opportunity to file a complaint against an exhumation order. In addition, the proposed amendment will extend the powers of the prosecutor to withdraw from the opening of the corpse. The applicants argue that existing constitutional standards, such as the right to court, the right to legal protection of private life or, finally, the right to challenge a decision, are in favour of the implementation of the proposed rules. And here it is not possible, in our opinion, to disagree with the objections that the families of the victims of the Smolensk disaster report against another one, carried out many years after the burial of their dead loved ones. The powerlessness of these people and the lack of any instruments to defend their relatives led the authors of the project to attempt to change the law in this regard. Respect for the corpse is an important element of European culture. For these reasons, the legal status of corpses not subject to classic legal qualifications is exceptional. In Polish law the subject of protection remains respect and honor for the remains of the dead and their resting places. In this case, protection is also followed by feelings of loved ones and possible religious feelings. In view of the fact that the process operation in the form of exhumation is contrary to the above-mentioned goods, i.e. in respect for human bodies and remains and in the feelings of loved ones, the possibility should be allowed - which is worth noting: the possibility - to verify the order to remove the corpse from the grave. We have one objection to the project. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 5 It shall be concluded that, in duly justified cases, the prosecutor or the court may waive the examination or opening of the corpse referred to in paragraph 1. The authors do not specify in any way the concept of justified cases, leaving in this respect the freedom of interpretation to the prosecutor and to the courts. In our appreciation, it is worth trying to clarify the wording in the course of our work. The introduction of arbitrariness in such sensitive and sensitive matter can lead to conflicts. In my opinion, this weak point in the undoubtedly good and necessary project can be improved during committee work. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I would like to appeal for understanding and compassion for the families of the victims of the Smolensk catastrophe. Let us give them the right to decide whether they agree to exhume the bodies of their loved ones, the more so that the purpose of these exhumations is widely questioned and questionable today. On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I would like to request that the project be submitted for further work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u298	Dear Marshal! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen! In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 209 § 1 is replaced by the following: If there is a suspicion of fatality, an examination and an opening of the corpse shall be carried out subject to § 6. These procedural actions are intended to explain the cause and circumstances of death. Failure to do so would make it impossible for the authority to ascertain correctly the facts of the case. The word "possibly" indicates the optional behaviour of the authority. The occurrence of a justified case does not automatically result in withdrawal from the action. The decision is still a matter for the Authority. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u299	Thank you very much. The first vote will be taken by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice, Mr Marcin Warchoł. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u300	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As the draft position of the government is still in consultation, I am pleased to present the draft of the Ministry of Justice. Proposed amendment of the Article. 209 and Art. 210 does not deserve to be taken into account for the following reasons. The purpose of criminal proceedings, one of the objectives, is to ensure that the perpetrator is, detected and held liable and that an innocent person does not take such responsibility. Therefore, it is the duty of the prosecutor to verify the existence of these conditions in the context of Article. 297 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, namely the objectives of the preparatory procedure. Accordingly, where there is a reasonable suspicion of criminal manslaughter, it is the responsibility of the prosecutor to carry out an inspection and to open the corpse. It is not known under what circumstances the prosecutor would have the right to waive this task assigned to both the Article. 2 as well as Art. 297 Code of Criminal Procedure. After all, we are talking about justified cases. We don't know what kind of cases that would be. Is this a question of verification of the grounds of felony manslaughter? After all, the prosecutor checks this condition by making his own decision to investigate and open the corpse. He's already verified it once. In that case, may he resign in duly justified cases? There's no internal logic here. As regards, in turn, the complaint referred to in the draft Article. 210 § 3, nor is it clear what the object of this complaint would be. The object of the complaint is the conditions in Article. 438 and Article 438 439 Code of Criminal Procedure, namely the so-called. relative and absolute causes of appeal, i.e. insulting material law, insulting procedural law. In this case, it is likely that only verification in this complaint of criminal manslaughter could be acceptable, and since so, it is the purpose of the entire criminal proceedings - the criminal manslaughter of death. And that's where the wheel closes. Projectors are investigating an internal contradiction. How can we verify the criminal cause of death in a complaint when this is the purpose of criminal proceedings? The point is that in order to verify this, we need to perform this operation without this action we will not verify it. If the State gives the prosecutor the opportunity to withdraw from this verification, then you deprive him of the possibility to verify this condition in the form of a criminal manslaughter, the type of crime referred to in Article. 148, Art. 155 Code of Criminal Procedure, e.g. Murder or involuntary manslaughter. At this point, These are crimes, the circumstances of which will simply not be possible. Moreover, as a general rule, the act of evidence is not subject to a complaint. Yes, there are situations like. control, recording of conversations where such a complaint is served - Article. 240 Code of Criminal Procedure. However, these are not actions that hold back, these are not complaints that hold back this action. These complaints are not absolutely suspensive. We have in the Code of Criminal Procedure one case of absolute suspense of complaint and this is a situation of complaint against the so-called. Order arrest, that's an extortion of detention as a punishment. And only there does a party's complaint stop the execution of that decision. However, this is not the case in the case of evidence. The party may not interfere with the course of the evidence. Therefore, it is not the authorities of preliminary or judicial proceedings, it is not the prosecutor or the court that would be responsible for the outcome of the trial, but the parties themselves would almost determine the outcome of the process they like. You might as well file complaints in a moment, e.g. stopping searches, complaints holding back many other actions of evidence, which, after all, is taken by the judicial authority for which this authority is responsible, because it has a duty to seek the determination of material truth, the principle of an appropriate response, etc. Therefore, this proposal cannot find ours as the Ministry of Justice's recognition. Finally, the last question. I did not mention the prohibition of evidence, speaking of the fact that the Polish Code of Criminal Procedure does not in principle know complaints about the actions of evidence. Yes, there are exceptions such as those referred to in Article. 240. While we have such a complaint with the prohibition of evidence, e.g. Article 2 180 § 2 - complaint for the hearing of a lawyer. However, there are clear indications: if this action cannot be done in any other way or was aimed at the good of the justice system, then we can only question the lawyer as a witness. However, please note that this action may be replaced by another action, here we will not replace it with anything, they are so-called. unique activities, e.g. removal of the corpse from the grave. This is a classic action unique, irreplaceable nothing, no other evidence. So the parties can throw out an important instrument for determining the criminal liability of the accused by their actions. Therefore, in our recognition this project is not only contrary to the whole principle of criminal liability, the verification of this criminal liability in the course of criminal proceedings, but is also internally contradictory. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Leszek Skiba to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u304	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to you a government bill amending the Personal Income Tax Act, no. 1258 Sejm printing. The aim of the project is, first, to free taxpayers of income tax on individuals earning income exclusively from payers or pension bodies from making their own tax returns, second, to reduce the costs associated with collecting income tax on individuals PIT by reducing the costs associated with supplying tax authorities with paper copies of statements and handling paper declarations. The proposal proposes changes in the way in which personal income tax is settled for taxable persons earning income exclusively from payers - it is proposed that this possibility of annual income tax settlement of individuals should be extended to personal clearing taxpayers, together with their spouse, in the manner provided for single parents, as well as to pension bodies. This change would be that in the case of taxpayers - pensioners - who do not benefit from any deductions and who wish to transfer 1% the tax payable to a selected public benefit organisation, it is proposed to introduce the possibility of making a declaration to such a taxable person indicating the public benefit organisation to which the taxable person wishes to transfer 1% the tax due. The statement made by the taxpayer, together with information on the income received from the pension authority held by the tax office, would be equivalent to the submission of the tax return. A request for a statement would only be made electronically. The declaration can be made in both paper and electronic form. In order to submit an application or an electronic statement, the taxable person could use either the tax portal or the e-declaration system or the national bank providing electronic services. The application or statement could be signed by the amount of income shown in the tax return for the previous year or by a qualified signature or by a trusted profile, or by means of the authentication data used by the national bank to verify electronically the bank account holder. Introduction changes will not close the way for taxpayers to settle their personal income tax in the currently available way. Taxpayers who do not wish to benefit from the proposed new form of settlement will be able to continue to settle with the tax office by submitting a tax return in paper or electronic form. In view of the fact that 16.5 million taxpayers are obliged to submit a tax return each year, it is proposed that the change introduced in the accounting of personal income tax may already apply to income obtained from 1 January 2016. It is assumed that in 2017 the proposed method for the settlement of personal income tax could benefit up to 25% taxpayers making tax returns on PIT-37 and in subsequent years an increase in the number of persons using this form of settlement would be around 10% per year. The proposed amendment can contribute by eliminating costs to the creation of savings on the part of the public finance sector of about PLN 20 million over a period of 6 years after the changes were made. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u320	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Legislative Commission a report on the Government draft law on specific rules for the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate issued in violation of the law. These are 1056 and 1214 prints. As the applicants point out, the aim of the project is to restore the protection of the social interest by removing infringements that occurred in the course of the adoption of decisions based on the so-called provisions. Decree of Bierut, i.e. Decree of 26 October 1945 on the ownership and use of land in the capital city of Warsaw. The Sejm at its 32nd meeting on 13 December pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the draft law to the Legislative Commission for consideration. The committee met on 12 and 18 December. Written opinions for the bill were presented by: Supreme Court, National Chamber of Chambers, National Council of Notarials, National Representation of Local Government Colleges of Appeals. The committee was attended by the Secretary of State in the Ministry of Justice, Patrick Jak, and the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Justice, Marcin Warchoł, together with colleagues, a representative of the Attorney General Tomasz Górski, as well as employees of the Sejm Chancellery, the Registry of the Legislative Commission and the Legislative Office. The result of the committee's work is the draft law included in the report in print no. 1214. The project was presented by the Secretary of State, Patrick Jak. All the regulations of the project have been systematically discussed and adopted at the meetings of the Legislative Commission. The opinions submitted and during the discussions raised doubts as to the appropriateness of the submission, as well as certain institutions and regulations of the project. As a result of the discussion, due to the amendments notified, some of the doubts were eliminated or mitigated. 10 substantive amendments have been adopted and a number of legislative amendments have been adopted. The substantive amendments concerned, inter alia,: create a standard of competence for the prosecutor to keep the case confidential when disclosure of the circumstances could jeopardise the good of the preparatory procedure; resolve doubts as to the competence to safeguard in the form of entries in the perpetual ledger after referral to the authority which issued the final reprivatisation decision; or arrange the grounds for amending the reprivatisation decision. In addition, and this seems important, amendments were adopted that the revenue due to the capital city of Warsaw for the reimbursement of undue benefits would be used to pay compensation and redress to victims of actions so called. Cleaners of tenement houses, and the committee has been given the power to grant such compensation or compensation to these people. Part of the amendments tabled were rejected, mainly on the grounds that they did not meet the objective indicated by the applicant. Their content is in principle in line with the minority proposals presented in print no. 1214. 11 Members voted in favour of the bill, 3 against, no abstentions. After considering the draft law, the Legislative Commission asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law as proposed in the committee's report. According to Art. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm at the request of the applicants, the committee shall submit 14 minority proposals. I would like to thank the ladies and gentlemen for their cooperation, as well as all the experts and participants in the committee's work. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u326	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Kukiz club15 relating to the report of the Legislative Commission on the Government draft law on specific rules for the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate issued in violation of law, print No. 1056. The project has already been carefully analysed here many times, so I will try to quote a few things briefly as part of the introduction. This draft law regulates the special procedure for dealing with the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate issued in violation of the law, determines the competent authorities for this procedure and the civil effects of decisions taken in that procedure. The law creates a committee, because an important element of this law is the creation of a committee whose task is also to appear to the competent authorities in the course of proceedings before the committee of the existence of circumstances conducive to the issuing of reprivatisation decisions in violation of the law or the committing of crimes or hindering their disclosure. The Commission has the status of a public-interest body - at least that is the case. The chairman of the committee is to be appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers, i.e. the de facto representative of the government, and eight members appointed by the Sejm are to be appointed. Minister... In fact, the rules of procedure of the committee are defined by the Minister of Justice. At the same time, at the committee there is a social council, a nine-member social advisory council, the members of which will also be appointed by the Minister of Justice in agreement with the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration. And what is very important, this committee has very great powers, in principle it has all the powers of the committee of inquiry, of the Parliament's committee of inquiry, so in principle, I would say why we should be balancing open doors, since we have an instrument in the form of the possibility of setting up a parliamentary committee of inquiry to do so, but on the other hand, these powers are also greatly extended, even in such a way that this committee is beginning to have a little bit of quasi-court power, which seems to indicate that it is also entering the powers reserved for the judiciary, e.g. handling of judgments, etc. Without denying the need to take radical steps to resolve the so-called case. wild reprivatisation - the word "reprivatisation" is quite abused here, because it is the lack of reprivatisation that has caused the state that we are dealing with - and at the same time the elimination of the effects of illegal activities, but we must also not forget to maintain such fundamental principles of good law or, I would say, the rule of law. In our view, the project raises serious doubts about its constitutionality. We also have important doubts as to whether the legal effects arising from the activities created in the draft committee will later not be subject to review, or whether they may be affected. These doubts we have show that maybe the courts won't recognize them. Another thing. At the same time, we believe that the committee according to this proposal becomes, as I mentioned, a quasi-court. It is to have a very high level of competence, but it is, in our opinion, lacking the qualities of independence and impartiality. Moreover, it becomes, as indicated here, a purely governmental body, and Parliament's role is kind of secondary, and we have always stood in the position that this audit role, that is to say the running of committees of inquiry, is, however, an element reserved for the Sejm, for Parliament. We really do not see the need to create a very strange, unknown to date and a number of doubts about the creation, while not using existing proven instruments, I will once again remind you, such as the Sejm Committee of Investigation, especially since the draft committee will basically only address a certain selected element, we are only talking about the so-called. wild Warsaw reprivatization, while we all know that this is the tip of the iceberg and it does not really solve the problem. From the beginning, we have made it clear that obvious crimes or crimes at all should be dealt with by the prosecution, which currently seems to have all the tools and at the same time is so controlled by the government that its efficiency can be monitored at all times when it comes to detecting and explaining these cases. And the whole so-called thing. wild reprivatisation, to a full territorial extent, i.e. covering the whole country, as well as political responsibility for irregularities, crimes and the creation of conditions for them, should be addressed by a parliamentary committee of inquiry, which has all the necessary powers to clarify the whole of the problem, and will also address the aspect of political responsibility. Ms Krystyna Górska has been operating since 1965 in inter-legitimate culture. She coordinated the entire cultural and educational work in the field until the physical uprising, building an object of an interlegated cultural house. Infinitely devoted to theatrical, social and cultural activities, she served as an instructor in the district's cultural center in the district of Międzychodzki. Thanks to her training and promotion of theatre culture in the 1980s, children's and puppet theaters flourished. In total, 30 theatres were created in the municipality of Międzychod, which were directed, instructed and supported by Mrs Krystyna. Theatrical bands were known and admired throughout the Gorzów Voivodeship, as well as beyond its borders. Krystyna Górska devoted her life to spreading culture in the smallest corners of the district. Miss Krystyna Górska, apart from her work to spread culture, loved children. With them in mind, she founded a branch of the Society of Friends of Children in the Inter-East, which continues to operate dynamically until today. The Children's Friends' Society, TPD, is an association, a non-governmental organization operating public benefit for children in the form of volunteering. In the long term of its activity - in 2 years' time the Society of Friends Children in Międzychodzi will celebrate 60 years of existence and in Poland 100 years - it has been sacrificial for children, especially children requiring help, who have fought for the welfare of children who are at risk of alcohol, drugs, nicotine, sick and disabled. TPD supports parents, local government and state authorities in fulfilling their responsibilities towards children and young people. It has in its work a major work mainly thanks to donors and people of good will, who are not indifferent to the fate and welfare of our children. ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u368	Statement on the 150th anniversary of the birth of General Mariusz Zaruski. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On the last day of January, we celebrated the 150th anniversary of the birth of the outstanding individual - General Mariusz Zaruski. He was a versatile man: military, taternik, pioneer of Polish sailing, painter, rescuer, scout instructor of ZHP and founder of the Tatra Volunteer Rescue Service. Mariusz Zaruski was born on January 31, 1867 in Podol. He studied at a private school and later in the Russian middle school in Kamieniec Podolski, where he was taken under the care of a conspiracy uncle. In 1885. Zaruski graduated and started his math and physical studies at the University of Odessa. That's when he first met the sea. First he fixed them on the canvas, and he decided to join the School of Fine Arts, and then during the holiday he would enlist on ships and make exotic trips. In 1894 he was sent to Archangielska for patriotic activities. He graduated from Maritime School there and got permission to travel. After the mission, he returned to Odessa, where he got married and then went to Cracow and graduated from the Academy of Fine Arts. When his wife got sick, he moved to Zakopane and took care of the training of guides, the setting of trails, as well as the work in the TOPR, which he initiated the uprising and the first warden. When World War I broke out, Zaruski was a well-known activist and publicist. From 1914 to July 1917 served in 1. The U.S. Regiment. Since November 1918, he has been his commander for almost three years. He was also the leader of the war in 1920. He also got the task of forming 11. The U.S. Regiment and the command of the U.S. Army. He was awarded the Order of the Virtuti Militari V class and several times with the Cross of Valiant Struggles. He ended his military career as Brigadier General and Adjutant of President Stanisław Wojciechowski in 1923 and 1925. He was rested before the turn of May. In his retirement he devoted himself completely to propaganda and organizational activity in order for Poles to understand the value of the sea. He organized the Yacht Club of Poland and was the creator of the National Fleet Committee. In the 1930s, the general was a leading figure in Polish sailing, the creator of many initiatives and undisputed authority. In August 1939 he was about to embark on a cruise, but he did not want to leave his homeland in need. He didn't want to save himself, the crew and the ship, he chose to stay at the station. That's why he was arrested by the NKVD. He fell to hell and died in 1941 away from the country, in the prison of the NKVD in the city of Kherson. In 1997 he was decorated posthumously with the Great Order of Rebirth of Poland. In the same year, they were brought to Poland and placed in the Old Cemetery in Zakopane a urn with land from 10 places of the cemetery in Kherson (the exact burial site is unknown). In April 2001, on the 60th anniversary of the General's death, a symbolic sea funeral was organized. The earth with the ashes of Mariusz Zaruski was sprinkled from the deck of the STS. General Zaruski to the water in the Gulf of Gdansk, on the approach to Gdynia - the home port of the Black Vistula. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-02-08-sejm-35-1.u369	Statement on the increasing phenomenon of e-addiction. Dear Marshal! In today's statement, I would like to draw attention to the growing scale of e-dependence. Along with technological progress and all the facilities that we make for digitalisation, we can encounter a flaw in e-addictment. This phenomenon is progressing from year to year, as more and more of our vital functions are moving to the cyber world. The dependence on new technologies and the Internet, especially among young people, is becoming increasingly noticeable and noticeable. This is confirmed by the results of public opinion studies and scientific studies. Many people at a young age can no longer imagine living without being on-line, which often does not end only by using a mobile phone or belonging to a social network. The recent control of the Supreme Audit Chamber has shown that a noticeable failure in the fight against e-addiction is the lack of guidance on how to identify and diagnose abuse of electronic media by children and young people and on how to address e-addiction. In the report, NIK also pointed out that schools or psychology and pedagogical clinics, which were the first individuals to help parents see and solve the problem of abuse of electronic media, did not make a reliable diagnosis of the school environment for abuse by students of electronic media and thus did not have information about the number of students affected by the problem. Given the scale of the progressing phenomenon, I ask the High House to take urgent action that will contribute to better communication and later cooperation, which will counter e-dependence. Finally, the final remark in the NIK report is very important, indicating the need to provide standardised diagnostic tools for psychological and pedagogical counselling in the field of diagnosing e-dependence. Thank you very much.
